# Authoring delta

- Baseline: three Level 3 runs of the frozen brief on cli-devin swe-2-max, recorded in `../001-template-drift-census-and-baseline/scratch/baseline-swe-l3/baseline.json`.
- Final: three runs of the same task, recorded in `scratch/baseline/baseline.json`.
- Each cell is the minimum, the maximum and the mean over three runs.
- Three runs cannot support a significance claim. The table reports a range, not an effect size.

| Metric | Baseline min | Baseline max | Baseline mean | Final min | Final max | Final mean | Delta of means |
|--------|--------------|--------------|---------------|-----------|-----------|------------|----------------|
| strict_passed | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| leftover_hints | 0 | 1 | 0.7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | -0.7 |
| missing_anchors | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 6 | 2 | 2 |
| out_of_scope_writes | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seconds | 317 | 384 | 342.3 | 429 | 1822 | 896 | 553.7 |

# system-spec-kit Benchmark Sources

> system-spec-kit · model-eval · authoring-baseline

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This map names where the raw evidence behind this folder lives. The folder holds curated outputs only.

| Resource | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Target skill | `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit` |
| Source packet | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring` |
| Baseline runs | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/001-template-drift-census-and-baseline/scratch/baseline-swe-l3/baseline.json` |
| Final runs | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/baseline/baseline.json` |
| Per-run extras | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/baseline/runs-meta.jsonl` |
| Run and metric script | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/tools/run-final.sh` |
| Delta | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/baseline/delta.json` |
| Run briefs and replies | `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/briefs/` and `scratch/runs/` |
| Model | cli-devin `swe-2-max` |

## 2. BOUNDARY

The corpus is an input and is never rewritten by a run. This folder holds outputs only. A run whose result changes gets a new folder rather than overwriting this one.

title: "system-spec-kit v2.9.0.0, templates a weak model can follow"
description: "New packets ship every hint in the five core templates as a value token, the validator gates an unfilled token after Draft, each core section says how to fill it, and a short authoring card replaces the long guide as the first read. One benchmark criterion failed and is recorded here."

# v2.9.0.0, Templates a Weak Model Can Follow

A weak model can now see what a template still needs, because every hint in the five core templates is a value token that fails validation once the packet leaves Draft. The four authoring intents also load a short card rather than the long guide, and the examples no longer teach the defects the old text carried. The before and after benchmark found fewer leftover hints and one failed criterion, described below.

> Spec folder: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring` (a phased packet of Level 3 phases)

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **An unfilled hint can no longer pass as finished.** A doc stamped v2.3 warns while the spec is Draft and fails strict validation at any other status.
- **Every core section says how to fill it.** Each section carries a short instruction and a good and weak example.
- **Authoring starts on one page.** The router loads a short card for the four authoring intents.
- **The examples stop teaching the old defects.** Their descriptions, stale checklist lines and anchors now match the templates.
- **Commands and agents name the new gate and the card.** A speckit or doctor run describes the same authoring path a worker follows.
- **The playbook and catalog cover the new behavior.** A focused run of eight scenarios passed on a disposable worktree.
- **The result is measured, and one criterion failed.** Three runs of one frozen Level 3 authoring task on each side gave a range for five metrics. Leftover hints fell from a worst case of 1 to 0 and every run passed strict validation. One final run deleted the six optional Level 3 verification sections of `tasks.md`, so the missing-anchor metric failed (6 against 0). Against the anchors the contract requires, all six runs miss none, and the follow-up is to make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors and rerun the benchmark.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Existing packets keep their verdict, because the token gate applies only to docs stamped v2.3. Scaffold a new packet to get the new templates and the authoring card.

# Changelog

## 2026-10-24

> Spec folder: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/003-versioned-placeholder-gate` (Level 3)
> Parent packet: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring`

### Summary

An unfilled template hint now fails strict validation once a new packet leaves Draft, and only warns while it is Draft. Older packets keep the verdict they had.

### Added

- The five core templates now hold 217 `[YOUR_VALUE_HERE: ...]` tokens in place of bare bracketed hints, and their stamps read v2.3.
- A lifecycle in the orchestrator and in the shell rule: a v2.3 doc warns on an unfilled token while `spec.md` is Draft and errors at any other status.
- `placeholder-token-gate.vitest.ts`, five tests: four pinned cells and a parity check between the orchestrator and the shell rule.
- A lifecycle section in the validation rules reference.

### Changed

- Five manifest entries moved to v2.3.
- Two existing tests and the scaffold snapshot moved to the v2.3 stamp.
- A doc with an older stamp keeps the error it always had, and `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION:` is an error everywhere.

### Fixed

- The angle-bracket form of the token is deferred the same way in both checkers. The orchestrator matched it and the shell rule did not, so a Draft doc used to warn in one and pass silently in the other.
- The repo era report counts only stamps older than v2.2 as legacy, so the manifest bump no longer inflates the pre-v4 count.

### Verification

- Strict validation of this phase folder: RESULT: PASSED
- Failing test first, then green after the code change, and three scripted breaks turned it red again
- Census over 4688 folders: 4496 PASS and 192 FAIL before and after, identical per folder
- Suite: matching counts plus the new test, exit=0
- Review: 1 finding, applied

### Files Changed

| File | Action | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| `templates/core/*.tmpl` (4) and `templates/addons/acceptance-criteria.md.tmpl` | Modified | Hints converted to tokens, stamped v2.3 |
| `templates/spec-kit-docs.json` | Modified | Five manifest entries moved to v2.3 |
| `runtime/lib/validation/orchestrator.ts` | Modified | Stamp and Draft lifecycle in the placeholder check |
| `runtime/cli/rules/check-placeholders.sh` | Modified | The same lifecycle in the shell rule |
| `runtime/cli/tests/placeholder-token-gate.vitest.ts` | Created | Five gate tests |
| `references/templates/template-guide.md, references/validation/validation-rules.md` | Modified | Guide corrected, lifecycle section added |

### Follow-Ups

- The post-edit scanner misses a hint inside a fenced block and a token with a colon. This phase records both and fixes neither.
- `upgrade-level.sh` copies tokens into older docs, so a v2.2 doc that gains a template section then holds tokens that error.
- `README.md` and the template mapping asset still say `[PLACEHOLDER]` and are left to the documentation phase.

# 2026-10-25--model-eval--authoring-baseline

> system-spec-kit · model-eval · authoring-baseline · cli-devin swe-2-max

---

## 1. HEADLINE / OVERVIEW

**Verdict: FAIL**

Three baseline runs and three final runs of one frozen Level 3 authoring brief. Every final run passed strict validation, and leftover hints run from 0 to 1 on the baseline side and from 0 to 0 on the final side. The verdict is FAIL because the worst final run is worse than the worst baseline run on missing_anchors: a final maximum of 6 against 0. The verdict is PASS only when every final run passes strict validation and none is worse than the worst baseline run on leftover hints, missing anchors or out-of-scope writes.

C5 FAIL on the frozen metric, and the verdict stays FAIL. Run 3 deleted the six optional Level 3 verification sections in `tasks.md`: `arch-verify`, `perf-verify`, `deploy-ready`, `compliance-verify`, `docs-verify` and `sign-off`.

Diagnostic, chosen after the result and not a verdict. Scored against the anchors the Level 3 `tasks.md` contract requires (`template-structure.js contract 3 tasks.md`: 15 required, and these 6 optional), all six runs, three baseline and three final, miss 0 required anchors. The six deleted sections are the optional ones. Three of the deletions went against the template's own fill notes: the packet records ADR-001 (`plan.md` line 239), it states NFR-P01 (`spec.md` line 133), and it holds paired documents. The run's reply says 'L3+ optional sections dropped (this is Level 3, not 3+)', so it read the `## L3+:` heading prefix as meaning Level 3+ only. Leftover hints improved from a baseline maximum of 1 to 0. Run 3 took 1822 seconds against 437 and 429 for the other two, and the cause is unknown.

The operator chose to keep the frozen metric and the FAIL, and to add this diagnostic ("Option 3 + diagnostic").

---

## 2. AGGREGATE RESULTS

| Metric | Baseline min | Baseline max | Baseline mean | Final min | Final max | Final mean | Delta of means |
|--------|--------------|--------------|---------------|-----------|-----------|------------|----------------|
| strict_passed | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| leftover_hints | 0 | 1 | 0.7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | -0.7 |
| missing_anchors | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 6 | 2 | 2 |
| out_of_scope_writes | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seconds | 317 | 384 | 342.3 | 429 | 1822 | 896 | 553.7 |

Each cell comes from three runs. The per-run rows are in `results.csv`.

---

## 3. METHODOLOGY

Fixture: one Level 3 packet for a `--quiet` flag on `recommend-level.sh`. The brief is `brief-1.txt` from the SWE 2 Max baseline, copied for each final run and compared with `cmp`, so no word changed. Each side is three serial runs by cli-devin `swe-2-max` on `--permission-mode dangerous` with a 2700 second watchdog. The measured documents are `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `tasks.md` and `acceptance-criteria.md`. The throwaway track is copied outside the worktree and then deleted after each measurement.

Metrics: `strict_passed` is the count of `RESULT: PASSED` lines from strict validation of the packet. `leftover_hints` counts bare v2.2 hint strings from the baseline list (121 patterns) plus v2.3 value tokens, so each unfilled instruction counts once whichever spelling the template used. `missing_anchors` sums the shortfall of each document against the baseline anchor counts (spec 7, plan 14, tasks 21, acceptance-criteria 3). `out_of_scope_writes` counts new lines in the git status after the run, outside the bench track and this phase folder. `seconds` is the dispatch end second minus the dispatch start second, the same clock the baseline used.

Each run's Devin session was found by working directory and start window, and its tool calls were searched in both the call and the update column for the baseline evidence folder, this phase's scratch folder and `/private/tmp`.

---

## 4. PER-CANDIDATE PROFILES

| Side | Tree | What the worker saw |
|---|---|---|
| baseline | Commit `ac2be31ec6`, before the packet changes | v2.2 templates, bare bracketed hints, the hand-written examples |
| final | Commit `a15bc0636e`, after phases 1 to 8 | v2.3 value tokens, section fill guidance, the authoring card, the guards |

---

## 5. PROCESS NOTES

Final runs 1 to 3 ran in order with nothing else touching the worktree. No attempt was discarded, and every dispatch exited with status 0. The audit found exactly one session per run and no tool call naming a forbidden path. The git diff of `.skilled` hashed the same before and after each run, and the status diff showed no write outside the bench track.

The three final runs took 437, 429, 1822 seconds. The three baseline runs took 326, 317, 384 seconds.

The report file is `README.md`, not the `benchmark-report.md` that the benchmark authoring skill names, because every existing folder under this reports root holds `README.md` and the index links to the folder.

---

## 6. FINDINGS

The load-bearing number is `missing_anchors`. Its final maximum is 6 against 0 on the baseline.
Run 3 wrote a `tasks.md` with 15 anchors where the baseline expects 21. Compared with run 1, it lacks `arch-verify`, `perf-verify`, `deploy-ready`, `compliance-verify`, `docs-verify`, `sign-off`. Strict validation still passed, so no validator rule catches those sections.

Scored against the contract-required anchors, all six runs miss 0 of 15 required `tasks.md` anchors. The six anchors run 3 deleted are optional in the contract.

Leftover hints fell: the final maximum is 0 and the baseline maximum is 1. Strict validation passed in all 3 final runs. No run wrote outside its track.

Time rose. Seconds per run ranged from 429 to 1822 on the final side against 317 to 384 on the baseline. Two final runs took about as long as each other (437 and 429), and run 3 took 1822.

---

## 7. CAVEATS

Three runs per side cannot support a significance claim. One run produced the whole regression, so the sample cannot say whether the omission comes from the template change or from ordinary run-to-run variation. The fixture is one small feature and four documents. The model is one model at one setting. A template change and a card change landed together, so this report cannot separate their effects.

The final packets were copied to the conductor scratchpad outside the worktree and are not part of the repository. The raw run logs and audit files are in the source packet named in `source.md`.

---

## 8. RECOMMENDATIONS

- **Apply now.** Keep the shipped template work and report the result as it is: C5 FAIL on the frozen metric, fewer leftover hints, and one run that deleted six optional Level 3 verification sections.
- **Validate first.** Make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun this benchmark in a new phase with three or more runs on the same brief and see whether the omission recurs.
- **Future.** Decide whether strict validation should treat the optional Level 3 verification anchors as required when a packet records an ADR, a performance requirement or paired documents. That is a validator change and belongs to its own packet.

---

## 9. REPRODUCIBILITY

```bash
bash .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/create.sh --skip-branch --level 3 --track zz-bench-hints --short-name recommend-level-quiet-flag "Add a quiet flag to recommend-level.sh"
rg -o --no-filename "\[YOUR_VALUE_HERE: [^]]*\]" specs/zz-bench-hints/001-recommend-level-quiet-flag/*.md | sort -u > hints.txt
```

The dispatch and the five metric commands are in `scratch/tools/run-final.sh` in the source packet. Run it as `run-final.sh N dispatch` and then `run-final.sh N measure`. The three final runs took about 45 minutes together.

---

> Spec folder: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/001-template-drift-census-and-baseline` (Level 3)
> Parent packet: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring`

### Summary

Phase 001 produced the verified defect ledger and the first authoring baseline that phases 002 to 009 consume. That first baseline was three Level 2 runs on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, and a Level 3 rebaseline on cli-devin swe-2-max later replaced it.

### Added

- A defect ledger of 72 rows, each with an evidence command that reproduces on the start commit, covering 18 leaked example descriptions and the five confirmed defects.
- Three census lanes over the templates, examples and references, with a coverage check and a deterministic reduce that keeps only rows that reproduce.
- A three-run authoring baseline with five metrics per run, and a hint list for later comparison.
- A frontier review of the ledger with 47 findings, each recorded in the phase goal log.

### Changed

- Two ledger rows changed after review: `L-C-003` was removed as a false positive and `L-C-013` had its expected text corrected.

### Fixed

- No product file changed in this phase. The 45 other review findings were recorded for the owner phases instead of applied here.

### Verification

- Strict validation of this phase folder: RESULT: PASSED
- Ledger: 72 rows, 0 schema failures, 72 of 72 evidence commands reproduce
- Baseline: 3 runs, each with 6 keys
- Review disposition: 47 findings, 47 recorded lines
- Suite: finished with exit=0

### Files Changed

| File | Action | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| `spec.md, plan.md, tasks.md, acceptance-criteria.md, goal.md, implementation-summary.md` | Modified | Phase plan, tasks and close-out evidence |
| `graph-metadata.json` | Modified | Refreshed derived metadata after the source-doc edits |

### Follow-Ups

- Three runs detect only large effects, so later phases report a minimum and a maximum instead of a significance claim.
- 45 review findings are recorded, not applied. They need owner-phase decisions or exact replacement text.
- The Level 2 DeepSeek baseline is history. The packet compares against the Level 3 cli-devin swe-2-max baseline.

## 2026-10-25

> Spec folder: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring` (a phased packet of Level 3 phases)

### Summary

A weak model can now author a filled spec packet from the system-spec-kit templates: an unfilled value token fails strict validation after Draft, every core section says how to fill it, a short card is the first read, and the examples no longer teach the old defects. The packet measured the result on a frozen Level 3 task with three runs per side. Leftover hints fell and every run passed strict validation, but parent criterion 5 failed on the frozen metric because one run deleted six optional Level 3 verification sections.

### Included Phases

| Phase | Status | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| `001-template-drift-census-and-baseline` | Complete | Verified defect ledger and the first authoring baseline |
| `002-template-and-example-defect-repair` | Complete | Example descriptions, checklist and style guide repaired by exact text swaps |
| `003-versioned-placeholder-gate` | Complete | Unfilled value tokens fail after Draft for v2.3 docs only |
| `004-template-section-fill-guidance` | Complete | A fill line with a good and a weak example in every section, and the example drift guard |
| `005-weak-model-authoring-card` | Complete | The authoring card, the router change and the card sync guard |
| `006-speckit-command-and-agent-alignment` | Complete | Commands and agents point at the card, the token and the guards |
| `007-playbook-catalog-readme-alignment` | Complete | Eight scenarios, four catalog leaves and the doc edits |
| `008-playbook-run-in-worktree` | Complete | All eight scenarios passed on a disposable worktree |
| `009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout` | In Progress | Benchmark run, report and close-out records, with criterion 5 FAIL |

### Added

- The v2.3 value-token gate: a doc stamped v2.3 warns on an unfilled token while the spec is Draft and errors at any other status, and older docs keep their verdict.
- A fill line with a good and a weak example in every core and addon template section.
- A short authoring card that the four authoring intents load instead of the long template guide.
- Two guards, `check-example-drift.sh` and `check-card-sync.sh`.
- Eight playbook scenarios and four catalog leaves, run on a disposable worktree with eight passes.
- A benchmark report of the before and after for five metrics, with verdict FAIL.

### Changed

- The speckit commands, the doctor entries and the doc-authoring agents name the new gate, the card and the guards.
- The playbook, catalog and READMEs describe the new authoring path.
- The parent executor and commit lines now state one local commit per phase and a Claude Code conductor.

### Fixed

- 18 example descriptions, stale checklist lines, a dead hash file and the write-recipe move destination.
- The repo era report no longer counts v2.2 stamps as pre-v4.

### Verification

- Recursive strict validation of the packet: RESULT: PASSED
- Parent criteria: C1, C2, C3, C4, C6 and C7 PASS, C5 FAIL
- Census over 4688 folders: 4496 PASS and 192 FAIL before and after, 0 differing rows
- Playbook run: 8 scenarios, 8 PASS
- Benchmark: missing_anchors final maximum 6 against a baseline maximum of 0, leftover_hints final maximum 0 against 1

### Files Changed

| File | Action | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/templates/` | Modified | Value tokens, fill lines and aligned examples |
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/` | Modified | Gate, guards and tests |
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/references/` | Modified | Authoring card and corrected references |
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/benchmark/reports/` | Created | Playbook run report and benchmark report |
| `.skilled/changelog/system-spec-kit/v2.9.0.0.md` | Created | Global changelog entry |

### Follow-Ups

- Parent criterion 5 is FAIL and the packet is In Progress. Make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun the benchmark in a new phase.
- Three runs per side cannot support a significance claim.

# Changelog

<!-- SPECKIT_TEMPLATE_SOURCE: changelog/phase.md | v1.0 -->

## 2026-10-24

> Spec folder: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/002-template-and-example-defect-repair` (Level 3)
> Parent packet: `specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring`

### Summary

A model that copies a system-spec-kit example or follows the authoring checklist no longer meets a broken line. The 18 example descriptions are whole sentences, the checklist names a packet that exists, the style guide says table in both places, the dead `templates/.hashes` file is gone and every ledger row owned by this phase is fixed.

### Added

- An exact-match applier that refuses to edit unless the old text occurs once, with a `DELETE LINE` form for the one row that removes a line.
- Four review-driven fixes for second copies of defects already repaired.

### Changed

- 18 example descriptions are now whole sentences, and the verification checklist line in the examples matches the template.
- Stale paths, folder names and command names are corrected in the template and workflow references.
- Three compiled deep-loop contracts and the scaffold golden snapshot are regenerated, because both record the changed text.
- Five ledger evidence commands now test the document text instead of a disk fact that stays true after the fix.

### Fixed

- The dead `templates/.hashes` file is deleted. It listed files that no longer exist and nothing read it.
- The checklist path in the authoring workflow now points at a packet that exists.
- The style guide wording is consistent in both places that mention tables.

### Verification

- Strict validation of this phase folder: RESULT: PASSED
- Known rows applied: 25 of 25 with no path outside the target
- Recheck after the review fixes: 0 residual hits
- Suite against the phase 001 start: matching summary lines on the fourth run, exit=0
- Review: 4 findings, all applied

### Files Changed

| File | Action | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| `templates/examples/level-*/` (18 docs) | Modified | Whole-sentence descriptions and the verification checklist line |
| `templates/README.md, templates/CONTRACT.md` and two more | Modified | Stale paths and folder names |
| `references/workflows/*.md, references/templates/*.md` (10 docs) | Modified | Checklist paths, style guide wording, stale command names |
| `templates/.hashes` | Deleted | Listed files that no longer exist |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/*.contract.markdown` (3) | Regenerated | New digest of the auto mode contract |
| `runtime/cli/tests/snapshots/scaffold-golden-snapshots.vitest.ts.snap` | Regenerated | New packet pointer lines for Levels 2, 3 and 3+ |

### Follow-Ups

- The suite needs more than its default time bound on a loaded machine. Later runs set `SPECKIT_TEST_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS=1800000`.
- The suite comparison reads summary lines only, so a differing line needs a read of the failing tests.

- [ ] Read `recommend-level.sh` before editing (READ FIRST — understand the parse loop and output dispatch)

### Execution Rules

| Rule | Description |
|------|-------------|
| TASK-SEQ | Complete tasks in order; T004 lands before T005 and T006; T008–T010 run only after implementation |
| TASK-SCOPE | Only `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/recommend-level.sh` may be modified; packet docs live under `specs/zz-bench-authoring/001-recommend-level-quiet-flag/` |
| CONTRACT | Without `--quiet`, stdout is byte-identical; with `--quiet`, stdout is exactly one line and errors stay on stderr |

### Status Reporting Format
After each task: `Task T### complete. Files modified: [list]. Verify: [command output]`

### Blocked Task Protocol
If blocked: stop, mark the task `[B]`, record the blocker and the failing command output beside it, and escalate — do not widen scope to work around it.

title: "Feature Specification: Add a quiet flag to recommend-level.sh"
description: "recommend-level.sh always prints a 16-line human report, so a caller that needs only the recommended level must parse prose. This packet adds a --quiet flag that prints just the recommended-level line."

<!-- SPECKIT_TEMPLATE_SOURCE: spec-core + level2-verify + level3-arch | v2.3 -->
# Feature Specification: Add a quiet flag to recommend-level.sh

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 3 -->


---

## EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

`recommend-level.sh` always prints a 16-line human report, so a caller that needs only the recommended level has to grep through prose. The change adds a `--quiet` flag that prints exactly the recommended-level line and nothing else. A `wc -l` count of `1` on quiet stdout, plus a byte-identical default run, proves the contract.

**Key Decisions**: `--quiet` is an output-mode selector like `--json`, and the two are mutually exclusive (passing both is a usage error, exit 1) | the quiet line reuses the exact `Recommended Level: N (Name)` string the text report already prints

**Critical Dependencies**: Only `bash` and the script's existing `determine_level` / `output_text` functions; no new external dependency

---

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Level** | 3 |

| **Created** | 2026-10-25 |
| **Branch** | `scaffold/001-recommend-level-quiet-flag` |
<!-- /ANCHOR:metadata -->

<!-- ANCHOR:problem -->
## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE

### Problem Statement
`recommend-level.sh` always prints a multi-line report: a blank line, the recommended-level line, a score line, a breakdown block and a phase block (16 lines for a typical run). An orchestrator or pipeline step that only needs the level must extract it from prose with `grep`/`sed`, which couples the caller to the report's layout.

### Purpose
With `--quiet`, stdout carries exactly one line — `Recommended Level: N (Name)` — so a caller can capture the answer directly as `$(recommend-level.sh ... --quiet)` without parsing the report.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:scope -->
## 3. SCOPE

### In Scope
- Add a `QUIET` global and a `--quiet` case arm to the option parser in `recommend-level.sh`
- Reject the `--quiet` + `--json` combination during input validation with a stderr message and exit 1
- Add a quiet output branch that prints only the `Recommended Level: N (Name)` line
- Document `--quiet` in `show_help` and in the header USAGE comment

### Out of Scope
- A `-q` short alias — the request names `--quiet` only; no short form is added
- Any change to the default text report, the JSON shape, scoring or exit codes — unchanged by definition
- Editing any file other than `recommend-level.sh`

### Files to Change

| File Path | Change Type | Description |
|-----------|-------------|-------------|
| `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/recommend-level.sh` | Modify | Add the `--quiet` flag, its validation conflict check, the quiet output branch and the help text |
<!-- /ANCHOR:scope -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:requirements -->
## 4. REQUIREMENTS

### P0 - Blockers (MUST complete)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| REQ-001 | The option parser accepts `--quiet` in any argv position and sets a `QUIET` flag |
| REQ-002 | With `--quiet`, stdout contains exactly one line: `Recommended Level: <N> (<Name>)` — the same text `output_text` prints |
| REQ-003 | Without `--quiet`, stdout is byte-identical to current behavior for every existing flag combination |
| REQ-004 | Passing `--quiet` together with `--json` (in either order) exits 1 with a stderr message naming the conflict and no stdout output |

### P1 - Required (complete OR user-approved deferral)

| ID | Requirement |
|----|-------------|
| REQ-005 | `--help` documents `--quiet` and its `--json` conflict |
| REQ-006 | Existing error paths (missing `--loc`/`--files`, non-numeric values) behave identically under `--quiet`: stderr message, exit 1, empty stdout |
| REQ-007 | `--quiet` composes with the scoring and phase flags (`--auth --api --db --architectural --no-recommend-phases --phase-threshold`); the quiet line reflects the same computed level |
| REQ-008 | `bash -n` syntax check passes and the script keeps `set -euo pipefail`, its shebang and its exit-code contract |

> Acceptance criteria for these requirements live in `acceptance-criteria.md`,
> which is the document that decides whether this packet may close.
<!-- /ANCHOR:requirements -->

<!-- ANCHOR:success-criteria -->
## 5. SUCCESS CRITERIA

- **SC-001**: `bash .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/recommend-level.sh --loc 500 --files 15 --api --quiet` prints exactly `Recommended Level: 2 (Verification)`, `wc -l` on stdout is `1`, exit code `0`
- **SC-002**: The same invocation without `--quiet` produces output byte-identical to a baseline captured before the change (`diff` prints nothing)
- **SC-003**: `recommend-level.sh --quiet --json --loc 100 --files 3` exits `1`, prints nothing to stdout, and stderr names the flag conflict
- **SC-004**: `recommend-level.sh --help` prints a `--quiet` line in its OPTIONS list
<!-- /ANCHOR:success-criteria -->

<!-- ANCHOR:risks -->
## 6. RISKS & DEPENDENCIES

| Type | Item | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|------|--------|------------|
| Dependency | `bash` (already required by the script) | None — no new dependency | N/A |
| Risk | A caller could already parse the report text; a quiet-mode edit might drift the shared level line | Low | REQ-002 pins the quiet line to the same `Recommended Level: N (Name)` string `output_text` prints; AC-002 diffs the default output against a captured baseline |
| Risk | `--quiet --json` has an ambiguous winner | Med | REQ-004 makes the combination an explicit usage error rather than silent precedence; recorded in plan.md ADR-001 |
| Dependency | `.skilled/commands/speckit/assets/speckit-plan.yaml` invokes the script with `--json` | Quiet must not alter JSON output | The JSON branch is untouched; the conflict error keeps the two output modes disjoint |
<!-- /ANCHOR:risks -->

---


## 7. NON-FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS

### Performance
- **NFR-P01**: Quiet mode adds one flag comparison and one `echo`; runtime is identical to the text path within noise (single-digit ms, same process)

### Security
- **NFR-S01**: No new input source, file read, credential or network access is introduced; `--quiet` is a boolean flag with no value

### Reliability
- **NFR-R01**: Exit-code contract is unchanged: `0` on success, `1` on usage error; errors still go to stderr only

---

## 8. EDGE CASES

### Data Boundaries
- Empty input: `--quiet` with neither `--loc` nor `--files` still hits the existing "At least --loc or --files must be provided" error — stderr, exit 1, empty stdout
- Maximum input: `--loc`/`--files` accept any non-negative integer; quiet output length is constant regardless of score magnitude

### Error Scenarios
- Conflicting modes: `--quiet --json` or `--json --quiet` exits 1 with `ERROR: --quiet cannot be combined with --json` on stderr
- Unknown flag: `--quiet --bogus` still hits the existing `ERROR: Unknown option` path, exit 1

### State Transitions
- Partial completion: N/A — the script is stateless; a failed validation exits before any output
- Session expiry: N/A — no session state exists

### Acceptance Scenarios
- **Given** the script is invoked, **When** `--quiet --help` are combined, **Then** the help text prints and the script exits 0 (the existing `--help` arm wins)
- **Given** `--quiet` is passed twice (`--quiet --quiet`), **When** the script runs, **Then** stdout still holds exactly one line (the flag is idempotent)

---

The feature's intrinsic score sits in Level 0-1 territory (the script's own recommender prints Level 0 for `--loc 15 --files 1`). This packet runs the Level 3 document contract because the dispatch instruction fixed `--level 3`; the score is reported honestly rather than inflated to match the level.

## 10. RISK MATRIX

| Risk ID | Description | Impact | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---------|-------------|--------|------------|------------|
| R-001 | Default report output drifts while adding the flag | M | L | AC-002 diffs a full-flag-combination run against a captured baseline; nothing outside the new branch may change |
| R-002 | `--quiet --json` ambiguity resolved inconsistently by argument order | M | L | REQ-004 rejects the combination at parse time; order-independent; ADR-001 records the decision |
| R-003 | A caller depends on the quiet line's exact text | L | M | The line reuses `output_text`'s string verbatim; AC-003 pins the anchored `Recommended Level: N (Name)` format |

## 11. USER STORIES

### US-001: Pipeline caller reads the level (Priority: P0)

**As a** pipeline step that gates on the recommended level, **I want** a one-line output mode, **so that** I can capture the level without parsing the prose report.

**Given** a caller runs `recommend-level.sh --loc 500 --files 15 --api --quiet`, **When** the script exits 0, **Then** stdout holds exactly `Recommended Level: 2 (Verification)`.

**Acceptance criteria:** see `acceptance-criteria.md` (rows referencing this story).

---

### US-002: CI step asserts on a single line (Priority: P0)

**As a** CI check, **I want** `--quiet` to guarantee one line of stdout, **so that** `wc -l` and a regex are a complete contract test.

**Given** any valid scoring input, **When** `--quiet` is passed, **Then** `wc -l` on stdout prints `1` and the line matches the pinned format.

**Acceptance criteria:** see `acceptance-criteria.md` (rows referencing this story).

---

### US-003: Existing report reader sees no change (Priority: P0)

**As a** user of the existing report, **I want** unchanged output when I omit the flag, **so that** my current parsing keeps working.

**Given** a run with no `--quiet`, **When** output is compared to the pre-change baseline, **Then** `diff` prints nothing.

**Acceptance criteria:** see `acceptance-criteria.md` (rows referencing this story).

---

### US-004: Operator gets a clear mode conflict error (Priority: P1)

**As an** operator who passed both output-mode flags, **I want** an explicit error, **so that** I am not silently given the wrong format.

**Given** `--quiet --json` on the command line, **When** validation runs, **Then** the script exits 1 and stderr names the conflict.

**Acceptance criteria:** see `acceptance-criteria.md` (rows referencing this story).

---

<!-- SPECKIT_TEMPLATE_SOURCE: plan-core | v2.3 -->
# Implementation Plan: Add a quiet flag to recommend-level.sh

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 3 -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:summary -->
## 1. SUMMARY

### Technical Context

| Aspect | Value |
|--------|-------|
| **Language/Stack** | Bash 3.2+ (single script, `set -euo pipefail`) |
| **Framework** | None — standalone CLI script |
| **Storage** | None — stateless; verification baselines go to the packet `scratch/` folder |
| **Testing** | Direct `bash` invocations: `bash -n` syntax check, `wc -l` line counts, `diff` against a captured baseline |

### Overview
The change adds one boolean flag to `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/recommend-level.sh`: a `QUIET` global, a `--quiet` case arm in the existing option parser, a mutual-exclusion check against `--json` in the validation block, a quiet branch in the output dispatch that prints `Recommended Level: ${RECOMMENDED_LEVEL} (${LEVEL_NAME})`, and matching help text. Scoring is untouched — quiet changes which output runs, not what is computed. Proof is a `wc -l` count of `1` in quiet mode and an empty `diff` between the default output and a pre-change baseline.
<!-- /ANCHOR:summary -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:quality-gates -->
## 2. QUALITY GATES

### Definition of Ready
- [x] Problem statement clear and scope documented (`spec.md` §2-§3)
- [x] Success criteria measurable (SC-001..SC-004 each name a command and expected output)
- [x] Dependencies identified (bash only; the `--json` consumer in `speckit-plan.yaml` is unchanged)

### Definition of Done
- [ ] All acceptance criteria met (AC-001..AC-008 in `acceptance-criteria.md`)
- [ ] Tests passing (`bash -n`, quiet line count = 1, baseline `diff` empty, conflict exits 1)
- [ ] Docs updated (spec/plan/tasks consistent; `--help` documents the flag)
<!-- /ANCHOR:quality-gates -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:architecture -->
## 3. ARCHITECTURE

### Pattern
Single-script output-mode branch — one flag selects between three emitters (quiet line / JSON document / text report).

### Key Components
- **Option parser (`while`/`case` loop)**: gains a `--quiet` arm that sets `QUIET=true`, beside the existing `--json|-j` arm
- **Validation block**: gains a mode-conflict check — `QUIET=true` and `JSON_OUTPUT=true` together exit 1 with `ERROR: --quiet cannot be combined with --json` on stderr
- **Output dispatch (section 7)**: `if [[ "$QUIET" = true ]]; then echo "Recommended Level: ${RECOMMENDED_LEVEL} (${LEVEL_NAME})"; elif [[ "$JSON_OUTPUT" = true ]]; then output_json; else output_text; fi`
- **`show_help` + header USAGE comment**: document `--quiet` and its conflict

### Data Flow
`argv` → parser sets `QUIET`/`JSON_OUTPUT`/scoring inputs → numeric validation → conflict check → `calculate_*_score`, `determine_level`, `determine_phasing` run unchanged → output dispatch selects quiet line, JSON, or text report → `exit 0`. Scores and phase results are computed identically in all modes; only the final emit differs.
<!-- /ANCHOR:architecture -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:affected-surfaces -->
## FIX ADDENDUM: AFFECTED SURFACES

| Surface | Current Role | Action | Verification |
|---------|--------------|--------|--------------|
| `recommend-level.sh` text path (`output_text`) | producer of the report | unchanged — quiet adds a sibling branch, it does not edit this function | `diff` of a default run against `scratch/baseline.txt` prints nothing |
| `recommend-level.sh` quiet branch | new producer of the single level line | update (added) | `... --quiet | wc -l` prints `1` |
| `recommend-level.sh` JSON path (`output_json`) | producer of machine output | unchanged | `speckit-plan.yaml`'s `--json` invocation returns the same JSON shape |
| `.skilled/commands/speckit/assets/speckit-plan.yaml` | consumer: calls the script with `--json --recommend-phases` | unchanged — never passes `--quiet`; conflict path unreachable from it | `rg -n 'recommend-level.sh' .skilled/commands` shows the same call |
| `references/workflows/spec-authoring-card.md`, skill README | documentation consumers | unchanged — they describe text/JSON modes accurately either way | manual read of the two files |

Required inventories:
- Same-class producers: `rg -n 'Recommended Level' .skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/runtime/cli/spec/recommend-level.sh` — only `output_text` emits the string; the quiet branch reuses it verbatim.
- Consumers of changed symbols: `rg -n 'recommend-level\.sh' .skilled/commands .skilled/skills --glob '*.yaml' --glob '*.sh' --glob '*.md'` — one programmatic caller (`speckit-plan.yaml`, `--json` mode) plus docs.
- Matrix axes: quiet × json × help × missing-input — the four rows are covered by AC-001, AC-004, AC-006, AC-008.
- Algorithm invariant: quiet mode must not alter the computed level — the quiet line is emitted from `RECOMMENDED_LEVEL`/`LEVEL_NAME` after the identical scoring path runs.
<!-- /ANCHOR:affected-surfaces -->

## L3: ARCHITECTURE DECISION RECORD

### ADR-001: `--quiet` and `--json` are mutually exclusive output modes

**Status**: Proposed

**Context**: The script ends with an output dispatch that selects `output_json` or `output_text`. Adding `--quiet` introduces a third output mode. A caller can legally pass both `--quiet` and `--json`, and a silent precedence rule (last flag wins, or one mode dominating) would let an orchestrator believe it received JSON when it received one line of text — or vice versa.

**Decision**: Treat `--quiet` and `--json` as conflicting output-mode selectors. When both are set, the validation block exits 1 with `ERROR: --quiet cannot be combined with --json` on stderr, in either argument order.

**Consequences**:
- Each output mode keeps an exact contract: quiet is always one line, JSON is always the full document
- Callers that genuinely want one machine-readable field discover the conflict immediately instead of silently parsing the wrong shape
- One extra validation branch; the conflict is a usage error that costs the caller one rerun — mitigated by documenting the conflict in `--help`

**Alternatives Rejected**:
- Quiet overrides `--json` (quiet wins silently): breaks the literal JSON contract for `speckit-plan.yaml`-style callers if `--quiet` is ever added upstream, and violates "the JSON document is the output" expectation
- Last-flag-wins ordering: makes identical logical invocations produce different output depending on argv order — a footgun with no benefit
- Emit `{"recommended_level": N}` under `--quiet --json`: invents a third output shape the feature never asked for; `--json` already serves machine consumers

## 1. DURABLE DIRECTIVE

| ID | Decision |
|----|----------|

| D3 | Three final runs, serial, on cli-devin `swe-2-max`, compared with the 3 `swe-2-max` baseline runs. No run is dropped when it is bad. |

### Deviations and findings

| Item | Note |
|------|------|
| Report folder name and the kebab checker | `check_authored_name_kebab.py` rejects the benchmark grammar `date--subject--variant`, because the checker allows a single hyphen between words. The benchmark skill requires the double hyphen, so the report uses the skill grammar and T008 checks it with its own pattern. |
| Report file name | `sk-create-benchmark` names `benchmark-report.md`. Every existing folder under `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/benchmark/reports/` holds `README.md`, and the index links to the folder. This phase writes `README.md` and keeps the ten-section contract. |
| Nested changelog file names | The generator prints `changelog-054-root.md` and `changelog-054-009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout.md` on this tree. The workflow reference describes the shape more loosely, so the generator output wins and the literal names in this phase use it. |
| Three runs cannot support a significance claim | The report prints a range and says so. A reader who needs an effect size needs more runs. |
| Bracket scan false positives | The bracket-hint scan matches the status format token in `plan.md` and the folder-name regex in `tasks.md`. Both sit inside fenced code blocks and are intentional literals, not unfilled hints. |
| Takeover on 2026-10-24 | The parent moved every remaining run to cli-codex gpt-6-luna max fast and cli-devin swe-2-max, and every review to swe-2-max (parent D4, D7, D8). A task that names the Task tool runs as that CLI dispatch with the same brief and reply file. |
| Level 3 retarget | The parent moved the benchmark to a Level 3 task on cli-devin swe-2-max with a no-regression criterion 5. T002 and T004 to T008 and the C5 proof now read the baseline, the brief, `hints-l3.txt` and `anchors-l3.txt` from phase 1 `scratch/baseline-swe-l3/`. Where `spec.md` or `plan.md` still name the Level 2 task, `hints-v2.2.txt`, `anchors-v2.3.txt` as the M3 list, the count of 97 or DeepSeek, `tasks.md` wins |
| D7 and the parent commit rule | D7 says nothing is committed. The parent's last criterion and the operator's takeover decisions allow local commits on the build worktree, so this phase ends with one local commit and never pushes. The parent wins by precedence |
| T014 precondition rows | The OpenCode run already set parent preconditions 2 and 4 to Done, and the takeover added two rows beside them. T014 checks those rows as they stand instead of rewriting them from Pending. |

| Run protocol on 2026-10-25 | The runs mirror the script that produced the SWE 2 Max baseline. Each dispatch uses the baseline brief-1.txt copied byte for byte (cmp), runs under a 2700 second watchdog on cli-devin swe-2-max with --permission-mode dangerous, and records its exit status. M5 is the dispatch end second minus the dispatch start second, as in the baseline, not the measurement time. M2 is the bare hint count (hints-l3.txt) plus the value token count. M4 is the status diff, and a change in the hash of git diff -- .skilled before and after a run also counts as an out-of-scope write. Each run's packet is copied to the scratchpad outside the worktree before it is removed. The session audit selects Devin sessions in the worktree between the start second and the end second plus 60, requires exactly one, and searches both tool_call_json and tool_call_update_json. A rate-limit or billing reply is not a run and is redone for the same run number. ### Status values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `Met` | Verified. The Verification cell names evidence that was actually observed. |
| `Unmet` | Not yet satisfied. Blocks closure. |
| `Waived` | Deliberately not pursued. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |
| `Superseded` | Replaced by a different criterion or decision. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |

### Waiver cell

Write `-` when the row is `Met` or `Unmet`. Write `ADR-NNN` when the row is
`Waived` or `Superseded`, naming a decision record that exists in
`decision-record.md`. A waiver naming an ADR that is not there fails validation:
the point of a waiver is that someone recorded the reasoning, so an unbacked
waiver is treated as an unmet criterion rather than as a pass.

# Implementation Summary

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 3 -->
<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

<!-- ANCHOR:what-built -->
## What Was Built

The packet now ends with a measured before and after, and the measurement found one failure. Three final runs of the frozen Level 3 authoring task are recorded beside the SWE 2 Max baseline. Every final run passed strict validation, the final maximum of `leftover_hints` is 0 against a baseline maximum of 1, and no run wrote outside its track. Parent criterion 5 is FAIL on the frozen metric, because run 3 left 15 anchors in `tasks.md` where the baseline expects 21, so `missing_anchors` has a final maximum of 6 against 0.

The six anchors run 3 deleted are the optional Level 3 verification sections: `arch-verify`, `perf-verify`, `deploy-ready`, `compliance-verify`, `docs-verify` and `sign-off`. A diagnostic, chosen after the result and not a verdict, scores all six runs against the anchors the contract requires (`template-structure.js contract 3 tasks.md` lists 15 required and these 6 optional), and every run misses none. Three of the deletions went against the template's own fill notes, because the packet records ADR-001, states NFR-P01 and holds paired documents. The run's reply says "L3+ optional sections dropped (this is Level 3, not 3+)", so it read the `## L3+:` heading prefix as Level 3+ only. Run 3 also took 1822 seconds against 437 and 429, and the cause is unknown. The run log is `scratch/baseline/runs.jsonl`, the extras are in `runs-meta.jsonl` and the delta is `scratch/baseline/delta.json`.

### Phase 9: authoring-benchmark-and-closeout

The closeout also published the benchmark report with verdict FAIL, wrote the global changelog `v2.9.0.0` and bumped the version carrier, wrote the nested changelogs for phases 001 to 003 and this phase, regenerated the trigger index, confirmed the track roots and reconciled the parent phase map and goal log. `scratch/closeout/criteria.txt` holds one line for each parent criterion: six PASS lines and one FAIL line, `C5 FAIL`.

<!-- ANCHOR:how-delivered -->
## How It Was Delivered

One Claude Code worker ran the phase and spawned no subagent. It ran three serial cli-devin swe-2-max dispatches of the frozen Level 3 brief and audited each run's Devin session. It measured each run with the five commands recorded in `scratch/tools/run-final.sh`, wrote every number in the report from `delta.json` and the run files, and proved each parent criterion before ticking any box. The census for criterion 3 was rerun over the frozen folder list, which excludes this packet. One read-only frontier review on cli-codex gpt-6-luna read the closeout diff, and each finding is recorded in `goal.md` section 4. The operator moved the review from the cli-devin swe-2-max that parent D4 names.

### Operator handoff

- This phase made one local commit on the worktree branch. Nothing was pushed.
- The follow-up is open: make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun the benchmark in a new phase.
- The parent `spec.md` and this phase are In Progress, not Complete, until that rerun passes criterion 5.
- No generated artifact needs a hand edit before review.
<!-- /ANCHOR:how-delivered -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:decisions -->
## Key Decisions

| Decision | Why |
|----------|-----|
| Keep the frozen metric and the FAIL, and add a labelled diagnostic | The operator chose "Option 3 + diagnostic". Changing the metric after the result would hide the finding |
| Count bare hints plus value tokens for M2 | The two patterns are disjoint, so each unfilled instruction counts once on both sides |
| Use Level 3 for the final runs | The SWE 2 Max baseline is Level 3 |
| Prove parent criterion 3 on the frozen folder list | A live walk would compare different populations |
| Do not mark the phase or the parent Complete | The validator reads a Complete status with an unmet acceptance row as a failed closure |
<!-- /ANCHOR:decisions -->

<!-- ANCHOR:limitations -->
## Known Limitations

1. **Parent criterion 5 is FAIL.** `missing_anchors` has a final maximum of 6 against a baseline maximum of 0, and the phase and the parent stay In Progress.
2. **Open follow-up.** Make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun the benchmark in a new phase.
3. **Three runs per side cannot support a significance claim.** One run produced the whole failure, so the sample cannot separate the template change from run-to-run variation.
4. **The fixture is one small feature and four documents.** A different feature shape or a Level 1 packet is not measured.
5. **Acceptance rows AC-010, AC-011 and AC-015 are Unmet.** They need nine Complete map rows, seven PASS lines and a Complete status, which criterion 5 prevents.
<!-- /ANCHOR:limitations -->

# Implementation Summary

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 3 -->
<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

| **Level** | 3 |
<!-- /ANCHOR:metadata -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:what-built -->
## What Was Built

The packet now ends with a measured before and after, and the measurement found one failure. Three final runs of the frozen Level 3 authoring task are recorded beside the SWE 2 Max baseline. Every final run passed strict validation, the final maximum of `leftover_hints` is 0 against a baseline maximum of 1, and no run wrote outside its track. Parent criterion 5 is FAIL on the frozen metric, because run 3 left 15 anchors in `tasks.md` where the baseline expects 21, so `missing_anchors` has a final maximum of 6 against 0.

The six anchors run 3 deleted are the optional Level 3 verification sections: `arch-verify`, `perf-verify`, `deploy-ready`, `compliance-verify`, `docs-verify` and `sign-off`. A diagnostic, chosen after the result and not a verdict, scores all six runs against the anchors the contract requires (`template-structure.js contract 3 tasks.md` lists 15 required and these 6 optional), and every run misses none. Three of the deletions went against the template's own fill notes, because the packet records ADR-001, states NFR-P01 and holds paired documents. The run's reply says "L3+ optional sections dropped (this is Level 3, not 3+)", so it read the `## L3+:` heading prefix as Level 3+ only. Run 3 also took 1822 seconds against 437 and 429, and the cause is unknown. The run log is `scratch/baseline/runs.jsonl`, the extras are in `runs-meta.jsonl` and the delta is `scratch/baseline/delta.json`.

### Phase 9: authoring-benchmark-and-closeout

The closeout also published the benchmark report with verdict FAIL, wrote the global changelog `v2.9.0.0` and bumped the version carrier, wrote the nested changelogs for phases 001 to 003 and this phase, regenerated the trigger index, confirmed the track roots and reconciled the parent phase map and goal log. `scratch/closeout/criteria.txt` holds one line for each parent criterion: six PASS lines and one FAIL line, `C5 FAIL`.

<!-- ANCHOR:how-delivered -->
## How It Was Delivered

One Claude Code worker ran the phase and spawned no subagent. It ran three serial cli-devin swe-2-max dispatches of the frozen Level 3 brief and audited each run's Devin session. It measured each run with the five commands recorded in `scratch/tools/run-final.sh`, wrote every number in the report from `delta.json` and the run files, and proved each parent criterion before ticking any box. The census for criterion 3 was rerun over the frozen folder list, which excludes this packet. One read-only frontier review on cli-codex gpt-6-luna read the closeout diff, and each finding is recorded in `goal.md` section 4. The operator moved the review from the cli-devin swe-2-max that parent D4 names.

### Operator handoff

- This phase made one local commit on the worktree branch. Nothing was pushed.
- The follow-up is open: make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun the benchmark in a new phase.
- The parent `spec.md` and this phase are In Progress, not Complete, until that rerun passes criterion 5.
- No generated artifact needs a hand edit before review.
<!-- /ANCHOR:how-delivered -->

<!-- ANCHOR:decisions -->
## Key Decisions

| Decision | Why |
|----------|-----|
| Keep the frozen metric and the FAIL, and add a labelled diagnostic | The operator chose "Option 3 + diagnostic". Changing the metric after the result would hide the finding |
| Count bare hints plus value tokens for M2 | The two patterns are disjoint, so each unfilled instruction counts once on both sides |
| Use Level 3 for the final runs | The SWE 2 Max baseline is Level 3 |
| Prove parent criterion 3 on the frozen folder list | A live walk would compare different populations |
| Do not mark the phase or the parent Complete | The validator reads a Complete status with an unmet acceptance row as a failed closure |

<!-- ANCHOR:limitations -->
## Known Limitations

1. **Parent criterion 5 is FAIL.** `missing_anchors` has a final maximum of 6 against a baseline maximum of 0, and the phase and the parent stay In Progress.
2. **Open follow-up.** Make the `## L3+:` heading label unambiguous for Level 3 authors, then rerun the benchmark in a new phase.
3. **Three runs per side cannot support a significance claim.** One run produced the whole failure, so the sample cannot separate the template change from run-to-run variation.
4. **The fixture is one small feature and four documents.** A different feature shape or a Level 1 packet is not measured.
5. **Acceptance rows AC-010, AC-011 and AC-015 are Unmet.** They need nine Complete map rows, seven PASS lines and a Complete status, which criterion 5 prevents.
<!-- /ANCHOR:limitations -->

### Status values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `Met` | Verified. The Verification cell names evidence that was actually observed. |
| `Unmet` | Not yet satisfied. Blocks closure. |
| `Waived` | Deliberately not pursued. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |
| `Superseded` | Replaced by a different criterion or decision. Requires an ADR in the Waiver cell. |

### Waiver cell

Write `-` when the row is `Met` or `Unmet`. Write `ADR-NNN` when the row is
`Waived` or `Superseded`, naming a decision record that exists in
`decision-record.md`. A waiver naming an ADR that is not there fails validation:
the point of a waiver is that someone recorded the reasoning, so an unbacked
waiver is treated as an unmet criterion rather than as a pass.
<!-- /ANCHOR:criteria -->

## 3. CLOSURE STATEMENT

**Closeable:** No

The phase has not started. This statement is rewritten when the phase closes.
<!-- /ANCHOR:closure -->

   233	<!-- /ANCHOR:milestones -->
   234	
   235	---
   236	
   237	## L3: ARCHITECTURE DECISION RECORD
   238	
   239	### ADR-001: `--quiet` and `--json` are mutually exclusive output modes
   240	
   241	**Status**: Proposed
   242	
   243	**Context**: The script ends with an output dispatch that selects `output_json` or `output_text`. Adding `--quiet` introduces a third output mode. A caller can legally pass both `--quiet` and `--json`, and a silent precedence rule (last flag wins, or one mode dominating) would let an orchestrator believe it received JSON when it received one line of text — or vice versa.
   244	
   245	**Decision**: Treat `--quiet` and `--json` as conflicting output-mode selectors. When both are set, the validation block exits 1 with `ERROR: --quiet cannot be combined with --json` on stderr, in either argument order.
   246

specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/review/T016-input/p009-implementation-summary.md:50:The packet now ends with a measured before and after, and the measurement found one failure. specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/review/T016-input/p009-implementation-summary.md-51-
specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/review/T016-input/p009-implementation-summary.md-52-The six anchors run 3 deleted are the optional Level 3 verification sections: `arch-verify`, `perf-verify`, `deploy-ready`, `compliance-verify`, `docs-verify` and `sign-off`. A diagnostic, chosen after the result and not a verdict, scores all six runs against the anchors the contract requires (`template-structure.js contract 3 tasks.md` lists 15 required and these 6 optional), and every run misses none. Three of the deletions went against the template's own fill notes, because the packet records ADR-001, states NFR-P01 and holds paired documents. The run's reply says "L3+ optional sections dropped (this is Level 3, not 3+)", so it read the `## L3+:` heading prefix as Level 3+ only. Run 3 also took 1822 seconds against 437 and 429, and the cause is unknown. The run log is `scratch/baseline/runs.jsonl`, the extras are in `runs-meta.jsonl` and the delta is `scratch/baseline/delta.json`.

Each dispatch uses the baseline brief-1.txt copied byte for byte (cmp), runs under a 2700 second watchdog on cli-devin swe-2-max with --permission-mode dangerous, and records its exit status. M5 is the dispatch end second minus the dispatch start second, as in the baseline, not the measurement time. M2 is the bare hint count (hints-l3.txt) plus the value token count. M4 is the status diff, and a change in the hash of git diff -- .skilled before and after a run also counts as an out-of-scope write. Each run's packet is copied to the scratchpad outside the worktree before it is removed. The session audit selects Devin sessions in the worktree between the start second and the end second plus 60, requires exactly one, and searches both tool_call_json and tool_call_update_json. A rate-limit or billing reply is not a run and is redone for the same run number. Extra per-run fields (exit status, bare and token split, audit) are in scratch/baseline/runs-meta.jsonl so baseline.json keeps the six keys per run. |

specs/system-speckit/054-weak-model-spec-authoring/009-authoring-benchmark-and-closeout/scratch/review/T016-input/p009-goal.md-113-| Run protocol on 2026-10-25 | The runs mirror the script that produced the SWE 2 Max baseline. Each dispatch uses the baseline brief-1.txt copied byte for byte (cmp), runs under a 2700 second watchdog on cli-devin swe-2-max with --permission-mode dangerous, and records its exit status. M5 is the dispatch end second minus the dispatch start second, as in the baseline, not the measurement time. M2 is the bare hint count (hints-l3.txt) plus the value token count. M4 is the status diff, and a change in the hash of git diff -- .skilled before and after a run also counts as an out-of-scope write. Each run's packet is copied to the scratchpad outside the worktree before it is removed. The session audit selects Devin sessions in the worktree between the start second and the end second plus 60, requires exactly one, and searches both tool_call_json and tool_call_update_json. A rate-limit or billing reply is not a run and is redone for the same run number. Extra per-run fields (exit status, bare and token split, audit) are in scratch/baseline/runs-meta.jsonl so baseline.json keeps the six keys per run. |
